 # v2.1.0.0, Completed Runs Can Be Extended
 
-A run that stopped at its iteration cap no longer has to start a new session to get more iterations. `--extend=N` reopens the finished run in place, so the same session and generation carry on and the earlier results stay on record.
+A run that stopped at its iteration cap no longer has to start a new session to get more iterations. `--extend=N` reopens the finished run in place. The same session and generation carry on. The earlier results stay on record.
 
 &nbsp;
 
@@ -22,7 +22,7 @@ A run that stopped at its iteration cap no longer has to start a new session to
 - **`--extend-minutes=M` adds time.** The flag raises the run's time budget by M minutes. Both flags accept positive integers only.
 - **Each extension is one ledger event.** The `run_extended` event records the extension, and the run re-synthesizes after the new iterations.
 - **The prior synthesis is kept.** The synthesis or review report is copied to a versioned snapshot before the first new iteration. An existing snapshot is never overwritten.
-- **Unsafe extensions are refused.** A run that is not complete, a fan-out config, or a value that is not a positive integer stops with a named error.
+- **Unsafe extensions are refused.** Three cases stop with a named error. They are a run that is not complete, a fan-out config and a value that is not a positive integer.

-`--depth-mode=expansive` is the one stop-path change that grows work instead of ending it. -`--depth-mode` takes `default` or `expansive`. One live review pilot ran. -`--depth-mode=expansive` grows a depth-capped frontier of sub-target nodes beside the review target. The value lands in `antiConvergence.depthMode`, and the caps live in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `budgetSplit` of 0.5/0.35/0.15 across the depth bands, and `maxFrontierNodes`, which is set to `maxIterations` when the config is created. +`--depth-mode=expansive` grows a depth-capped frontier of sub-target nodes beside the review target. The value lands in `antiConvergence.depthMode`, and the caps live in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `budgetSplit` of 0.5/0.35/0.15 across the depth bands, and `maxFrontierNodes`, which is set to `maxIterations` when the config is created. The mode is locked against the optimizer; the `expansiveDepth` block is in neither its tunable nor its locked list. An absent flag means `default`, which runs exactly as before.
-Checks are evaluated in this order (first match wins):
+Checks are evaluated in this order (first match wins), as `buildStopDecision` in `runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` runs them:
-1. **Max iterations** -- hard cap, always checked first.
-2. **All dimensions covered + clean** -- every configured dimension covered (the four by default), no active P0/P1, stabilization passed, gates passed.
-3. **Stuck detection** -- 2+ consecutive no-progress iterations.
-4. **Composite convergence** -- 3-signal weighted vote with threshold 0.60.
-5. **Legal-stop gate bundle** -- review-specific stop gates. If a stop vote fails any gate, persist `stopReason=blockedStop` and continue.
-6. **Default CONTINUE** -- none of the above triggered.
+1. **Max iterations** -- hard cap, always checked first. It outranks every veto below.
+2. **Consecutive failures** -- three trailing failed iterations stop the run with stop reason `error`.
+3. **Stuck detection** -- `stuckThreshold` (2) consecutive no-progress iterations route to stuck recovery.
+4. **Convergence mode off** -- `convergenceMode: off` returns `CONTINUE` (`convergenceModeOff`).
+5. **Stop policy max-iterations** -- `stopPolicy: max-iterations` returns `CONTINUE` (`stopPolicyMaxIterations`).
+6. **Stop candidate** -- all dimensions covered and clean (every configured dimension covered, the four by default, no active P0/P1, coverage stabilized) is a hard candidate; otherwise the 3-signal composite vote with threshold 0.60 is a soft one. No candidate returns `CONTINUE` (`noStopCandidate`).
+7. **Iteration floor** -- below `minIterations` the candidate returns `CONTINUE` (`minIterationsNotReached`).
+8. **Frontier veto** -- under `--depth-mode=expansive`, an open frontier node turns the candidate into `CONTINUE` (`frontierOpen`).
+9. **Legal-stop gate bundle** -- review-specific stop gates. If the candidate fails any gate, persist `stopReason=blockedStop` and continue.
+10. **Graph decision** -- the candidate is combined with the graph convergence verdict: `STOP_ALLOWED` stops, `STOP_BLOCKED` blocks the stop and `CONTINUE` continues the run.

+### Completed-run extension flags (--extend, --extend-minutes)
+#### Description
+The --extend=N and --extend-minutes=M flags on /deep:research and /deep:review reopen a completed single-executor run in place, and leave the registry-driven workflowMode, runtimeLoopType and backendKind classification unchanged.
+#### How It Works
+`mode-registry.json` has no extension field. The extension sets `maxIterations` to the iterations run so far plus N, keeps the session and generation, and is refused with fan-out. The behavior is documented in the research and review catalogs.
+#### Source Files
+See [`deep-loop-mode-classification/completed-run-extension-flag.md`](deep-loop-mode-classification/completed-run-extension-flag.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

diff --git a/README.md b/README.md
-Each pass runs in a fresh context against state kept on disk. A script, not the agent, decides when to stop. A review stays open while a P0 is active or a required check still fails, and it ends in a PASS, CONDITIONAL or FAIL verdict. A loop survives crashes and new sessions, because its progress lives in files.
+Each pass runs in a fresh context against state kept on disk. A script, not the agent, decides when to stop. A review stays open below its minimum iteration count, while a P0 is active or while a required check still fails, and it ends in a PASS, CONDITIONAL or FAIL verdict. A loop survives crashes and new sessions, because its progress lives in files.
+Two opt-in flags let a loop reach further. `--depth-mode=expansive` keeps a frontier of sub-questions or sub-targets and turns a stop into another pass while one stays open, though never past the iteration cap. `--extend=N` reopens a finished single-executor run in the same session with room for N more passes, and `--extend-minutes=M` adds time. Under the default stop policy it can re-stop at once, so pair it with `--convergence-mode=off` when the run must keep going. A review can also track custom dimensions beside its four defaults.

# Feature Specification: Playbook coverage, feature catalogs and alignment for the expansive-depth program

<!-- SPECKIT_LEVEL: 1 -->

<!-- ANCHOR:phase-context -->
## Phase Context

This is **Phase 14** of the expansive-depth program, and it runs last.

**Scope Boundary**: Documentation only. Operator decision 2026-11-17: each implementation phase writes its own manual testing playbook scenarios through `/create:manual-testing-playbook`. This phase verifies that coverage, updates the feature catalogs, runs the two alignment checks, fixes the doc findings and reruns. It changes no runtime script, command workflow or agent behavior.

**Dependencies**:
- All of `../001-completed-run-extension/` through `../013-no-redispatch-landed-iteration/` complete, so every documented behavior exists in code and every phase has written its scenarios.

**Deliverables**:
- A coverage check showing every row of the Feature to Playbook Map has a scenario its phase wrote.
- Updated feature catalog entries.
- A clean `/local-doc-alignment` report and a clean `/local-skilled-alignment` report.
<!-- /ANCHOR:phase-context -->

---

<!-- ANCHOR:problem -->
## 2. PROBLEM & PURPOSE
<!-- FILL: State the problem in two sentences: what breaks, and for whom. GOOD: The rotate-logs script never deletes old archives, so a host that copies `logs/archive` fills its disk. WEAK: The script has issues with output. -->

### Problem Statement
Phases 001-013 add `--extend=N`, `--depth-mode=expansive` for deep-research and deep-review, custom review dimensions and defect fixes that change what an operator sees, but no system-deep-loop manual testing playbook or feature catalog mentions any of them. An operator cannot find a scenario that exercises the new flags, and a reviewer cannot check the catalogs against the shipped code.

### Purpose
Confirm that every user-visible feature and fix of 001-013 has the playbook scenario its phase wrote, give each a catalog entry, and close the program with both alignment checks reporting no open finding.
<!-- /ANCHOR:problem -->

<!-- ANCHOR:measured-behavior -->
### Measured Behavior
<!-- FILL: Run the command this change affects and paste the command and its verbatim output. Quote every number the spec states about current behavior from this block. Write `N/A:` and a reason when no command applies. GOOD: `bash scripts/rotate-logs.sh` with 10 archives, then `ls logs/archive | wc -l` prints 10. WEAK: The script deletes old logs. -->
Run on `main` at 2026-11-17 from the repository root (`D=.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop`), before any of 001-013 is built.
```text
$ rg -l -- "--extend=|--depth-mode|depthMode" $D/manual-testing-playbook $D/deep-research/manual-testing-playbook $D/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook $D/runtime/manual-testing-playbook $D/feature-catalog $D/deep-research/feature-catalog $D/deep-review/feature-catalog $D/runtime/feature-catalog; echo "rg exit=$?"
rg exit=1
$ rg -il "custom review dimension" $D/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook $D/deep-review/feature-catalog; echo "rg exit=$?"
rg exit=1
```
No playbook or catalog file names either flag or custom review dimensions.
<!-- /ANCHOR:measured-behavior -->

---

<!-- ANCHOR:scope -->
## 3. SCOPE
<!-- FILL: List three to five things this change does, each starting with a verb. Before you list a new file, module, option, layer or dependency, name what fails today without it, or drop it. GOOD: Add a `--keep <n>` option that deletes all but the newest n archives in `logs/archive/`. WEAK: Update the script and docs. -->

### In Scope
- Verify playbook coverage: each row of the Feature to Playbook Map below has a scenario in its category, written by its owning phase through `/create:manual-testing-playbook`. A missing scenario is a finding for the owning phase, which adds it through the same command; this phase does not write scenarios.
- Update the system-deep-loop feature catalogs through `/create:feature-catalog system-deep-loop update --path <catalog dir>`.
- Run `/local-doc-alignment` on the expansive-depth topic and `/local-skilled-alignment` on the 001-013 change.
- Fix each doc finding: a playbook gap through its owning phase, a catalog finding through `/create:feature-catalog`, and a finding in a reference doc, `README.md`, `SKILL.md` or command doc by a direct edit here. Regenerate a compiled contract when a fix changes a document it digests. Rerun both checks until neither reports an open finding.

### Out of Scope
- Writing playbook scenarios. Each implementation phase owns its own (operator decision 2026-11-17).
- Runtime, command workflow or agent behavior changes. A finding that shows a behavior bug is a code finding: it is recorded in `goal.md` §4 and handed to the owning phase, which reopens with a fix task. This phase cannot close while a code finding is open, unless the operator records a waiver for it in `goal.md` §4.
- Changelog entries and release notes. Each implementation phase owns its own; `../scratch/cross-phase-handoff.md` lists which phases add one.

### Feature to Playbook Map

Category folders are where each owning phase writes its scenarios. The create command decides the final file names; this phase checks that a file exists in each category.

| Owning phase | User-visible behavior | Playbook category |
|-------|-----------------------|-------------------|
| 001 | `--extend=N` on a completed run | `deep-research/.../entry-points-and-modes/`, `deep-review/.../entry-points-and-modes/` |
| 002 | Research on `cli-claude-code` honors the configured Claude config dir | `runtime/.../executor/` |
| 003 | A review run never stops before `minIterations` | `deep-review/.../convergence-and-recovery/` |
| 004 | Next Focus ignores answered free text, review picks an uncovered dimension | `deep-research/.../iteration-execution-and-state-discipline/`, `deep-review/.../iteration-execution-and-state-discipline/` |
| 005 | The research prompt pack allows `research/research.md` and uses `Recommended Next Focus` | `runtime/.../prompt-rendering/` |
| 006 | The `findings_not_enumerated` gate and the `focusAlignment` guard run on real leaf fields | `deep-research/.../iteration-execution-and-state-discipline/` |
| 007 | Source diversity reads derived `quality_class` values | `runtime/.../coverage-graph/` |
| 008 | Custom review dimensions are tracked and can be covered | `deep-review/.../initialization-and-state-setup/`, `deep-review/.../review-depth-v2-rollout/` |
| 009 | A merged fan-out review keeps its coverage surfaces | `deep-review/.../fanout/` |
| 010 | `--depth-mode=expansive` on deep-research | `deep-research/.../entry-points-and-modes/`, `deep-research/.../convergence-and-recovery/` |
| 011 | `--depth-mode=expansive` on deep-review | `deep-review/.../entry-points-and-modes/`, `deep-review/.../fanout/` |
| 012 | Council seat cap of 3 per attempt and one quorum retry per pivot | `runtime/.../council/` |
| 013 | A landed iteration is not dispatched a second time | `runtime/.../executor/` |

### Files to Change

All paths sit under `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/` unless they start with `.skilled/commands/`.

| File Path | Change Type | Description |
|-----------|-------------|-------------|
| `deep-research/manual-testing-playbook/`, `deep-review/manual-testing-playbook/`, `runtime/manual-testing-playbook/`, `manual-testing-playbook/manual-testing-playbook.md` | Read | Coverage check only; the owning phases wrote the scenarios and the package indexes |
| `deep-research/feature-catalog/` | Modify | `loop-lifecycle/` and `convergence/` entries for the extension and the research frontier, written by `/create:feature-catalog` |
| `deep-review/feature-catalog/` | Modify | `review-dimensions/` and `loop-lifecycle/` entries for custom dimensions, the extension and the review frontier, written by `/create:feature-catalog` |
| `runtime/feature-catalog/` | Modify | `coverage-graph/`, `council/`, `executor/`, `prompt-rendering/` and `fanout/` entries for the runtime fixes, written by `/create:feature-catalog` |
| `feature-catalog/feature-catalog.md` | Modify | Hub catalog, for the `--depth-mode` flag beside the existing mode classification |
| Live docs an alignment finding names: `*/references/*.md`, `*/README.md`, `*/SKILL.md`, `.skilled/commands/deep/*.md` | Modify | Doc fixes only, each tied to a finding id |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/compiled/deep-*.contract.markdown` | Regenerate | Only when a doc fix changes a digested document; through `compile-command-contracts.cjs`, never by hand |
| This phase's `scratch/` | Create | Alignment run folders (`--out`) |
<!-- /ANCHOR:scope -->

## 6. RISKS & DEPENDENCIES

# Goal: Playbook coverage, feature catalogs and alignment for the expansive-depth program

<!-- SPECKIT_TEMPLATE_SOURCE: goal | v2.2 -->
<!-- HVR_REFERENCE: .skilled/skills/sk-doc/sk-create-with-human-voice/references/hvr-rules.md -->

<!-- ANCHOR:directive -->
## 1. DURABLE DIRECTIVE
<!-- FILL: Write one sentence: what the packet is for, not how. GOOD: Keep only the newest n archives on request. WEAK: Improve the script. -->

**Objective:** Confirm that every user-visible feature and fix of phases 001-013 has the manual testing playbook scenario its phase wrote, give each a feature catalog entry, and close the program with both alignment checks reporting no open finding.

### Decisions

Frozen choices. Changing one is an amendment.

| ID | Decision |
|----|----------|
| D1 | Each implementation phase writes its own playbook scenarios through `/create:manual-testing-playbook` (operator decision 2026-11-17); this phase only verifies coverage and routes a gap to its owning phase. |
| D2 | Catalog entries are written only through `/create:feature-catalog`; other doc findings are fixed here by direct edit, and a compiled contract they make stale is regenerated, never hand-edited. |
| D3 | This phase changes docs only; a code finding goes back to its owning phase and blocks closure until fixed or waived by the operator. |
| D4 | This phase starts only after phases 001-013 are complete. |

<!-- /ANCHOR:directive -->

---

<!-- ANCHOR:completion -->
## 3. COMPLETION CRITERIA
<!-- FILL: Write one command-checkable criterion per line. GOOD: `--keep 3` leaves 3 archives in `logs/archive` and exits 0. WEAK: The work is done. -->

- [x] `rg -l -- "--extend=" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/manual-testing-playbook .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook` prints at least one file in each directory.
- [x] `rg -l -- "--depth-mode=expansive" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/manual-testing-playbook .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook` prints at least one file in each directory.
- [x] `rg -il "custom review dimension" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/feature-catalog` prints at least one file in each directory.

# v4.1.0.0, Improved Chat Automation, Search & Session Handover

Long sessions now hand over cleanly, and Claude Code does it by default.

- Near its context limit, a session writes a handoff note and a fresh one picks up in the same terminal.
- The hop carries the running command, the goal and the session's name.
- Pi, OpenCode and Mistral Vibe now finish a hop too.

The skill advisor and the deep loops now say more and guess less.

- The advisor names the slash command behind a hub skill.
- It says whether the agent may run that command, start it or should ask first.
- Mistral Vibe joins as the eighth runtime, with Skilled's guards and advisor brief in every session.
- A deep review fails while a required check still fails, and a script makes that call.

Search, the spec commands and the overlay checks now report what really happened.

- Search answers say how much they left out.
- The spec commands save what they report and check their own work.
- Overlay checks in a vendored project refuse a bad write before it lands.

This release also changes what you install and what an agent may read.

- Skilled now needs a patched Node.js release.
- A new guard keeps the values in a `.env` file away from every agent, on all eight runtimes.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> Six changes can break an existing setup. Read these before you update.
>
> 1. Old session relaunch switch names are no longer read. [Old switch names](#c-old-switch-names-stop-working)
> 2. An open handoff note in the old state folder is not found. [Moving from session relaunch](#c-moving-from-session-relaunch)
> 3. The history lookup is no longer offered after a compaction. [Claimed note history](#c-a-claimed-note-offers-the-senders-history)
> 4. `/deep:ai-council` is gone, so run `/speckit:ai-council`. [AI Council](#c-the-ai-council-is-its-own-agent)
> 5. The standalone git pre-commit hook is gone. [Hook folders](#c-folders-say-what-they-guard)
> 6. Code Mode needs Node 24.22.1 or newer on 24. [Node.js](#c-patched-releases-are-required)

&nbsp;

## Why This Release

Several parts of Skilled said one thing and did another. Handover existed, but most people never had it on. Checks could pass while something underneath had failed. This release closes those gaps.

- Handover shipped off, so most Claude Code users never had it.
- A hop mid-command came back as a plain chat.
- The advisor named a skill, not the command to type.
- A deep review could pass while a required check failed.
- A search cut at its limit never said what it hid.
- An overlay re-pin could write a wrong value and still pass.
- Any agent could read `.env` and send its secrets to the model.
- Skilled still accepted Node 20 after an October security patch.

&nbsp;

| [Claude Code can keep your own hook copy in step](#c-claude-code-keeps-your-hook-copy-in-step) | One switch syncs Skilled's hooks into `~/.claude/settings.json`. | Installs and Overlays |
| [Spec commands save what they say they save](#c-spec-commands-do-what-they-report) | Their checks, review step and resume now match their own words. | Spec Workflow |
| [Agents ask only for what their tools can do](#c-the-orchestrator-sends-deep-work-to-its-command) | The orchestrator sends deep work to its command. | Agents |
| [A plain frontend request reaches the frontend mode](#c-frontend-requests-reach-the-frontend-mode) | `make the navbar sticky` now routes to the frontend mode of `sk-code`. | Code Skill |
| [Hook folders say what they guard](#c-folders-say-what-they-guard) | The audit log also hides passwords written inside a URL. | Hook Library |

&nbsp;

## Session Handover

Handover moves a long session to a fresh one before it runs out of context. The old session writes a note and ends, and a new session claims the note and carries on. This release turns it on for Claude Code by default and makes every hop carry more of the running state.

#### Claude Code Hands Over by Default <a id="c-claude-code-hands-over-by-default"></a>

- On for Claude Code when no switch is set.
- Codex, Pi, OpenCode, Cursor, Devin and Vibe stay off until you turn them on.
- Under `skilled-loop`, a session at 80% writes a note and ends.
- A fresh session claims the note in the same terminal.
- Without the wrapper, the session keeps the note and works on.
- Turn it off with `SYSTEM_SESSION_HANDOVER_CLAUDE_ENABLED=0`.

#### Old Switch Names Stop Working <a id="c-old-switch-names-stop-working"></a>

> [!WARNING]
> Old switch names are no longer read. `SYSTEM_SESSION_RELAUNCH_ENABLED=0` now does nothing, and Claude Code hands over again.

- Main switch: `SYSTEM_SESSION_HANDOVER_ENABLED`.
- Per runtime: such as `SYSTEM_SESSION_HANDOVER_CODEX_ENABLED`.
- Tuning variables: the `SESSION_HANDOVER_` prefix.
- Hook folder: `.skilled/hooks/session-handover/`.
- Seed: `[session-handover] claim and continue handoff`.
- `SYSTEM_IN_SESSION_HANDOVER_*` also works for on and off. The main name wins.
- `/doctor:env` names a conflict between the two.

> [!WARNING]
> An open note in the old state folder is not found. Move it as [Upgrade Notes](#c-moving-from-session-relaunch) says.

#### A Hop Carries the Command and the Goal <a id="c-a-hop-carries-the-command-and-the-goal"></a>

- The note carries an `Active command:` line.
- The next session reruns it with the same mode and arguments.
- Running workers are listed, so they never start twice.
- A Claude session with no goal hands on its packet's goal through `/goal`.
- A goal you met, failed or cleared stays ended.
- A goal that cannot travel prints one `goal not carried:` line.
- The session name carries over as `<title> (2)`, then `(3)`.
- The permission mode and flags such as `--add-dir` carry over too.

#### A Claimed Note Offers the Sender's History <a id="c-a-claimed-note-offers-the-senders-history"></a>

- `claim` prints a `## History Lookup` section after the note.
- Its one read-only command searches the sender's turns for 30 minutes.
- Claude Code, Codex, Mistral Vibe, Pi and OpenCode senders get it.

> [!WARNING]
> The lookup is no longer offered after a compaction. The OpenCode plugin `session-history-lookup.js` is gone.

#### Pi, OpenCode and Vibe Finish a Hop <a id="c-pi-opencode-and-vibe-finish-a-hop"></a>

- Pi and OpenCode finish the same hop as Claude Code.
- OpenCode 2 still drops a model you pick mid-session.
- Vibe hands over with `SYSTEM_SESSION_HANDOVER_VIBE_ENABLED=1` under `skilled-loop vibe`.
- Near its compaction point, a Vibe session writes a note and a fresh one claims it.
- `/speckit-handoff` under the wrapper arms a hop. Any other prompt calls it off.
- The wrapper warns when an exported `MISTRAL_API_KEY` would bill outside your Vibe plan.

<details>
<summary><b>Smaller fixes</b></summary>

- The shell shim runs only a wrapper it trusts. Add others to `SKILLED_LOOP_TRUSTED_CHECKOUTS`.
- No foreign folder or old process can plant a hop.
- A sub-agent cannot cancel or consume a handover.
- A waiting or failed hop no longer stalls. Your next prompt calls it off.
- A nudge arrives once.

---
title: "Completed-run extension flags (--extend, --extend-minutes)"
description: "The --extend=N and --extend-minutes=M flags on /deep:research and /deep:review reopen a completed single-executor run in place, and leave the registry-driven workflowMode, runtimeLoopType and backendKind classification unchanged."
trigger_phrases:
  - "--extend"
  - "--extend-minutes"
  - "completed run extension flag"
  - "extend a completed deep loop run"
version: 1.0.0.0
---

# Completed-run extension flags (--extend, --extend-minutes)

## 1. OVERVIEW

`--extend=N` and `--extend-minutes=M` are command-level options on `/deep:research` and `/deep:review`. They reopen a completed run in place. The flags are not part of `mode-registry.json`, so the hub classifies a request into the same workflow mode with or without them. The extension behavior lives in the research and review packets.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Hub Classification Unchanged

`mode-registry.json` carries the `workflowMode`, `runtimeLoopType` and `backendKind` discriminator for `research` and `review`, and it has no extension field. Routing resolves the same packet whether the flags are present or not.

### Flag Routing

The command workflow binds `--extend=N` as `extend` with lineage mode `completed-continue`, and `--extend-minutes=M` as `extend_minutes`. Both take positive integers, and `--extend-minutes` is valid only with `--extend`. The extension step sets `maxIterations` to the iterations run so far plus N and keeps the session and generation. A config with fan-out executors is refused before any lineage spawns.

### Packet Behavior

The extension behavior is documented in the packet catalogs: [completed-run extension for research](../../deep-research/feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/completed-run-extension.md) and [completed-run extension for review](../../deep-review/feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/completed-run-extension.md).

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/mode-registry.json` | Registry | Holds the discriminator for `research` and `review`, with no extension field. |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/research.md` | Command doc | The `--extend` and `--extend-minutes` flag rows for research. |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/review.md` | Command doc | The `--extend` and `--extend-minutes` flag rows for review. |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-research.yaml` | Workflow | `step_reject_extend_with_fanout` and `step_extend_completed_run` for research. |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml` | Workflow | `step_reject_extend_with_fanout` and `step_extend_completed_run` for review. |

### Validation And Tests

| File | Type | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/manual-testing-playbook/entry-points-and-modes/extend-completed-research-run.md` | Manual playbook | DR-065: `--extend=N` on a completed research run. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook/entry-points-and-modes/extend-completed-review-run.md` | Manual playbook | DRV-070: `--extend=N` on a completed review run. |

---

## 4. SOURCE METADATA

- Group: Deep-loop mode classification
- Canonical catalog source: `feature-catalog.md`
- Feature file path: `deep-loop-mode-classification/completed-run-extension-flag.md`

Related references:
- [deep-loop-mode-classification.md](deep-loop-mode-classification.md) - Registry-driven mode classification
- [depth-mode-flag.md](depth-mode-flag.md) - Depth mode flag
[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && echo "=== root README 250-305 ==="; sed -n '250,305p' README.md; echo "=== hub README 1-80 ==="; sed -n '1,80p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/README.md
=== root README 250-305 ===

The deep loops run unattended and stop only when their own evidence says the work is done. `/deep:research` investigates a question one focused pass at a time. Results grow in one research document inside the spec folder. `/deep:review` audits code in passes, ranks every finding P0, P1 or P2 and never edits the code it reviews.

Each pass runs in a fresh context against state kept on disk. A script, not the agent, decides when to stop. A review stays open below its minimum iteration count, while a P0 is active or while a required check still fails, and it ends in a PASS, CONDITIONAL or FAIL verdict. A loop survives crashes and new sessions, because its progress lives in files.

Two opt-in flags let a loop reach further. `--depth-mode=expansive` keeps a frontier of sub-questions or sub-targets and turns a stop into another pass while one stays open, though never past the iteration cap. `--extend=N` reopens a finished single-executor run in the same session with room for N more passes, and `--extend-minutes=M` adds time. Under the default stop policy it can re-stop at once, so pair it with `--convergence-mode=off` when the run must keep going. A review can also track custom dimensions beside its four defaults.

A loop can also spread across several AI CLIs at once. Each executor runs its own lineage, and for a review the strictest verdict wins the merge. A pool caps how many lineages run at once. A stall watchdog stops a lineage that stops making progress, and a cost cap bounds what each one spends. The [Deep Loop README](.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/README.md) describes both modes and the shared runtime.

### CLI Orchestration

Your main CLI does not have to handle every task itself. The CLI orchestration hub hands a task to another AI CLI on the same machine and reads the result back. The agent you are talking to stays in charge. It writes the brief, waits for the answer and checks it before using it.

This lets you mix subscriptions. You can work in one CLI and send research, reviews or bulk work to another, billed to that CLI's own plan. The bridges are provider agnostic. Each CLI runs whatever providers and models you have set up in it. That is why Skilled supports eight runtimes: whichever plans you already pay for, one runtime is your main CLI and the others receive the work you hand off.

You ask for it in plain words, for example "use cli-opencode to review this diff". The advisor routes the request to the matching bridge. The bridge checks that its CLI is installed and that the dispatch passes its hard rules, then runs it. The deep loops use the same bridges, so one research or review run can spread its passes across several models. [Runtimes And CLI Bridges](#10--runtimes-and-cli-bridges) lists all eight.

### Create And Update Skills, Agents And Commands

Extending Skilled follows the same framework that built it. The [sk-doc](.skilled/skills/sk-doc/README.md) hub holds a template, a set of standards and a validator for each kind of component. Ask for a new skill, agent or command in plain words and the advisor loads the matching sk-doc mode, so the result matches the rest of the repository.

Each kind also has a `/create` command for when you want the workflow directly. `/create:sk-skill` creates or updates a skill, and `/create:skill-parent` scaffolds a parent hub with its modes. `/create:agent` and `/create:command` cover agents and slash commands. `/create:readme`, `/create:changelog`, `/create:goal` and `/create:repo-rule` cover the documents around them. Every one runs with `:auto` or with `:confirm` to pause at each step.

Updating works the same way as creating. Name the component and the change, and the workflow edits it in place, checks it against its template and runs the sk-doc validator. A new skill needs no registration step, because the advisor finds it from its own metadata.

&nbsp;

## 6. 🧰 SKILLS

Fourteen skills ship with Skilled. Gate 2 loads one when the advisor matches it to a task. Seven of them are parent hubs. A hub holds several modes and routes each request to the mode that fits, 42 modes in all. The other seven skills work on their own.

### Parent Hubs

**[sk-code](.skilled/skills/sk-code/README.md)** writes, reviews and verifies code in the stack you are in. Its five modes are quality, for the checks after implementation, and review, for findings-first code review. CI covers GitHub Actions workflows and frontend covers CSS, HTML and browser work. The skilled mode holds the standards for this repository's own TypeScript, Python, shell and config code.

**[sk-doc](.skilled/skills/sk-doc/README.md)** authors documentation and components. Its fifteen modes create skills, parent hubs, READMEs, agents, commands, feature catalogs, manual testing playbooks, benchmarks, changelogs, diffs, frontmatter, repo rules and goals. Two more run quality control and apply the human voice rules.

**[sk-design](.skilled/skills/sk-design/SKILL.md)** turns one design identity into an artifact. Its three modes extract a measured style reference from a live site, draw a diagram and build a chart.

**[system-deep-loop](.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/README.md)** runs the autonomous loops over one shared runtime. Its two modes are deep research and deep review.

**[cli-external-orchestration](.skilled/skills/cli-external-orchestration/README.md)** hands work to another AI CLI. Its eight modes are bridges to OpenCode, Claude Code, Codex, Cursor, Devin, Pi, DeepSeek Harness and Mistral Vibe.

**[cli-classifier](.skilled/skills/cli-classifier/README.md)** asks a hosted classifier for typed judgments. It has one mode today, the Jev transport, and future classifiers join as new modes.

**[mcp-tooling](.skilled/skills/mcp-tooling/README.md)** bridges outside tools through Code Mode. Its eight modes cover Chrome DevTools, ClickUp, the Aside browser, Notion and Obsidian. Three more are read-only design sources: Figma, Refero and Mobbin.

### Standalone Skills

**[system-spec-kit](.skilled/skills/system-spec-kit/README.md)** owns spec folders, documentation levels, the validator, continuity and lexical search. Every file change goes through it.

**[system-skill-advisor](.skilled/skills/system-skill-advisor/README.md)** routes each prompt to the skill that fits, through a daemon-backed CLI.

**[system-skilled-doctor](.skilled/skills/system-skilled-doctor/README.md)** gets Skilled into your own project and keeps it working there. It owns eleven doctor commands for installing, linking, updating and checking a project, including the overlays that carry a copied project's own changes through each update.

**[sk-git](.skilled/skills/sk-git/README.md)** handles worktrees, conventional commits, pull requests and finishing a branch.

# system-deep-loop

> Run iterative research or an iterative code review as a loop that keeps its state on disk and stops on a convergence signal.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

### At a Glance

| Aspect | What it delivers |
|---|---|
| **Use it for** | Multi-round research or a multi-pass review. Each round builds on what the round before it found |
| **Invoke with** | `Skill(system-deep-loop)` with a mode hint. A `/deep:*` command such as `/deep:research` or `/deep:review` also reaches it |
| **Works on** | A research topic with web or code sources. A review target such as a spec folder or a file set |
| **Produces** | The selected mode's artifacts and convergence state under the target spec folder, such as `research/research.md` or `review/review-report.md` |

### What It Does

`system-deep-loop` is one routing hub for two active loops. A request for iterative investigation goes to the `deep-research/` packet, which writes `research/research.md` under the target spec folder. A request for an iterative audit or a release-readiness verdict goes to `deep-review/`, which writes `review/review-report.md` and returns a verdict. Each packet owns its convergence math, state files, artifacts and tool guards. The shared `runtime/` beneath both modes holds executor config, prompt-pack rendering, atomic state and the coverage graph.

One-shot retrieval belongs to `@context`. Implementation planning belongs to `/speckit:plan`. A single-pass review belongs to the code-review mode in `sk-code`.

### Why This Skill Exists

A deep-loop request can mean outward investigation or inward audit. The two loops share the same `runtime/` and the same append-only state path. One hub gives the family a single entry point, so the same request reaches the loop that owns it without a guess. The two packets still keep their own contracts, so one entry does not flatten the modes into one.

---

## 2. QUICK START

**Step 1: Invoke it.** Run `Skill(system-deep-loop)` with a mode hint such as `research: <request>`. You can also call `/deep:research` or `/deep:review`. Every surface reaches the same packet.

**Step 2: Check where a request routes.**

```bash
node .skilled/bin/compiled-route.cjs --hub system-deep-loop --prompt "deep review of the checklist changes"
```

Expected output: one JSON decision with `"action":"route"` and a target where `"workflowMode":"review"`. Exit code 0. Swap the prompt to `deep research on the routing drift` to see the research route.

**Step 3: Run the mode.** Pass the request to `/deep:review:auto "skill deep-review"`. The command workflow initializes `{spec_folder}/review/`, dispatches one review iteration per pass and synthesizes the report. The run produces `review-report.md` with a verdict, a findings registry, remediation workstreams and an audit appendix, plus the iteration files under `{spec_folder}/review/iterations/`.

---

## 3. HOW IT WORKS

Routing runs in two stages. Stage one picks the mode: the hub scores the request against the vocabulary classes in `hub-router.json`. The winning `workflowMode` selects one entry in `mode-registry.json`. Stage two picks the leaves: `ROUTER.md` maps the request's intent to packet-qualified reference files. Each path resolves to a canonical pair in `leaf-manifest.json`. The packet then runs its loop. It initializes state, dispatches one fresh agent per iteration, checks convergence and synthesizes its artifact. The hub only routes, so per-mode logic stays in the packet.

```mermaid
flowchart TD
  Request["Deep-loop request"] --> Hub["Hub scores the request"]
  Hub -->|research| Research["deep-research/ packet"]
  Hub -->|review| Review["deep-review/ packet"]
  Hub -->|no mode| Defer["defer: ask for the mode"]
  Research --> Runtime["runtime/"]
  Review --> Runtime
  Runtime --> Output["Artifacts and convergence state"]
```

### The Three-Tier Discriminator

Every registry entry carries three keys. `workflowMode` is the public mode key. Its values are `research` and `review`. `runtimeLoopType` is the convergence key that `runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` consumes. The router reads it from the registry rather than inferring it from `workflowMode`. `backendKind` names the backend that runs the mode, `runtime-loop-type` for both active modes. A custom mode would declare an explicit `null` and name its own backend path.

### One Advisor Identity

Each packet carries no `graph-metadata.json`. The hub root carries the only one, so the advisor sees one skill identity no matter which surface reaches a mode.

# system-deep-loop: Feature Catalog

This catalog inventories the live `system-deep-loop` hub surface. The hub classifies a request into one of two `workflowMode` packets (`research`, `review`) through a three-tier discriminator, holding no per-mode logic itself. A default-on, flag-gated compiled-routing fast path can resolve the same decision ahead of this registry-driven classification without changing what it resolves to.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

Use this catalog as the current-state inventory for the `system-deep-loop` hub. The hub classifies intent and loads the resolved packet; the `/deep:*` commands and native agent types are complementary surfaces that reach the same packets through their own static routers.

---

## 2. DEEP-LOOP MODE ROUTING

### Registry-Driven Deep-Loop Mode Classification

#### Description

`mode-registry.json` is the single source of truth for the hub's two `workflowMode` packets, each carrying a `workflowMode`/`runtimeLoopType`/`backendKind` three-tier discriminator.

#### Current Reality

`runtimeLoopType` is the graph-backed convergence key consumed by `runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs`; `backendKind` is `runtime-loop-type` (research/review). A second-layer surface router at the root `ROUTER.md` supports router-replay benchmarking without re-deciding the mode.

#### Source Files

See [`deep-loop-mode-classification/deep-loop-mode-classification.md`](deep-loop-mode-classification/deep-loop-mode-classification.md) for the full discriminator and source anchors.

---

### Depth mode flag (--depth-mode)

#### Description

The --depth-mode flag on /deep:research and /deep:review selects default or expansive depth, and leaves the registry-driven workflowMode, runtimeLoopType and backendKind classification unchanged.

#### How It Works

`mode-registry.json` has no depth field, and the runtime acts only on the expansive value. The behavior is documented in the research and review catalogs.

#### Source Files

See [`deep-loop-mode-classification/depth-mode-flag.md`](deep-loop-mode-classification/depth-mode-flag.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

---

### Completed-run extension flags (--extend, --extend-minutes)

#### Description

The --extend=N and --extend-minutes=M flags on /deep:research and /deep:review reopen a completed single-executor run in place, and leave the registry-driven workflowMode, runtimeLoopType and backendKind classification unchanged.

#### How It Works

`mode-registry.json` has no extension field. The extension sets `maxIterations` to the iterations run so far plus N, keeps the session and generation, and is refused with fan-out. The behavior is documented in the research and review catalogs.

#### Source Files

See [`deep-loop-mode-classification/completed-run-extension-flag.md`](deep-loop-mode-classification/completed-run-extension-flag.md) for full implementation and validation file listings.

---

## 3. FAN-OUT WRITE CONTAINMENT

### Fan-Out Write Containment

#### Description

How a fan-out keeps a dispatched lane's writes inside its own directory, and why the remedy is preservation rather than reversion.

#### Current Reality

Preservation is the default remedy and restore is opt-in per run. Every lane runs in the shared checkout, so the containment guard watches the checkout itself: an out-of-scope write stays on disk and is reported as an advisory or a violation. A burst of newly dirty paths between heartbeats proves a second writer and latches preservation even when restore was requested.

#### Source Files

See [fanout-write-containment/fanout-write-containment.md](fanout-write-containment/fanout-write-containment.md).

---

## 4. COMPILED ROUTING

### Compiled Routing And Legacy Fallback

#### Description

A default-on, flag-gated, additive directive in `system-deep-loop`'s `ROUTER.md` asks the compiled per-hub router contract to resolve the mode before falling through to the deep-loop mode classification above.

#### Current Reality

The directive is on by default for `system-deep-loop`, one of the seven activated hubs: with `SPECKIT_COMPILED_ROUTING` unset, `node .skilled/bin/compiled-route.cjs --hub system-deep-loop --prompt "<task>"` returns the authoritative decision and the hub follows it directly. Setting `SPECKIT_COMPILED_ROUTING=0` is the explicit kill-switch that forces legacy `mode-registry.json` classification; any error or a `{"servingAuthority":"legacy"}` sentinel also leaves routing unchanged.

#### Source Files

See [`compiled-routing-and-legacy-fallback/compiled-routing-and-legacy-fallback.md`](compiled-routing-and-legacy-fallback/compiled-routing-and-legacy-fallback.md) for resolution order, the tri-state flag, and serving-status anchors.

# Skilled Release Notes

> One entry per Skilled release, written for the operator who upgrades the framework.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This folder holds the framework's release notes, one entry per Skilled release. An entry's version is the git tag and GitHub release of the same number.

| Path | Holds |
|---|---|
| `v1+/`, `v2+/`, `v3+/` | Older entries, grouped by generation |
| `v4.0.0.0.md`, `v4.0.0.1.md`, `v4.0.0.2.md`, `v4.0.0.3.md` | Current generation entries at the top level |
| `v4.0.0.4.md` | Unreleased entry for the next release, with no tag or GitHub release yet |

An entry lives here when a GitHub release carries its version number. The entry whose tag does not exist yet is `v4.0.0.4.md`.

---

## 2. ADDING AN ENTRY

Run `/create:changelog skilled`. The workflow writes to this folder only when the operator names `skilled`.

Adding `--release` also tags `v<version>` and publishes the GitHub release, titled with the tag and the entry's editorial title.

House style: `v4.0.0.0.md` in this folder is the canonical exemplar that the changelog template points to.

---

## 3. VERSIONS

The scheme is `MAJOR.MINOR.SERIES.PATCH`. The version is also the release's git tag. Pass `--bump` explicitly for a release entry.

| `--bump` value | Bumps |
|---|---|
| `major` | MAJOR |
| `minor` | MINOR |
| `patch` | SERIES |
| `build` | PATCH |

---

## 4. RELATED

Each skill keeps its own changelog in `.skilled/skills/<skill>/changelog/`, reachable as `.skilled/changelog/<skill>/`. system-spec-kit's own history, for example, is in `.skilled/skills/system-spec-kit/changelog/`.

| Resource | Purpose |
|---|---|
| [`v4.0.0.0.md`](./v4.0.0.0.md) | Canonical exemplar for entry style |
| [`changelog-template.md`](../../skills/sk-doc/sk-create-changelog/assets/changelog-template.md) | Template every entry follows |

---
title: "DR-065 -- Completed-run extension with --extend"
description: "This scenario validates `--extend=N` on a completed deep-research run for `DR-065`. It focuses on same-session continuation, the `run_extended` ledger event and the synthesis snapshot taken before the first new dispatch."
version: 1.0.2.0
---

# DR-065 -- Completed-run extension with --extend

This document captures the realistic user-testing contract, current behavior, execution flow, source anchors, and metadata for `DR-065`.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This scenario validates completed-run extension for `DR-065`. It focuses on `--extend=N` reopening a completed research run in the same session and generation, recording the extension as one `run_extended` event, and copying the prior synthesis to a versioned snapshot before the first new iteration starts.

### Why This Matters

An operator who hits the iteration cap on a finished run should be able to buy N more iterations without starting a new session, losing the original session identifier, or overwriting the synthesis the run already produced. If the extension resets the generation, skips the snapshot, or writes a second extension event, the lineage stops being auditable.

---

## 2. SCENARIO CONTRACT

Operators run this as a real orchestrator-led check against a completed fixture run rather than a synthetic matrix. The scenario is only complete when the operator can explain, in plain language, how many iterations the extension added and where the prior synthesis went.

- Objective: Verify that `--extend=3` on a completed research run adds exactly three iterations in the same session and generation, records one `run_extended` event, and snapshots the prior synthesis.
- Real user request: The research run finished at its iteration cap. Give it three more iterations without starting a new session.
- Prompt: `Extend the completed deep-research run in the fixture spec folder by three iterations with --extend=3 --convergence-mode=off, so the converged run cannot re-stop at once, then confirm the original session identifier, the single run_extended event and the synthesis snapshot.`
- Expected execution process: Read the completed fixture config, run the research command with `--extend=3` against that spec folder, then read the state log, the config and the snapshot folder after the run finishes.
- Expected signals: Exactly one `run_extended` event appears, projected as one `extended` row in `research/deep-research-state.jsonl`. The config keeps the original `sessionId` and generation 1, `maxIterations` equals the prior iteration count plus 3, and the lineage mode is `completed-continue`. No `resumed` row is written for the extension. A file matching `synthesis-v*-r*.md` holds the prior synthesis, and exactly three new iteration files are written.
- Desired user-visible outcome: The user is told the run was extended by three iterations, that the same session continues, and where the prior synthesis was kept.
- Pass/fail: PASS if the event count, the session identifier, the iteration cap, the snapshot and the dispatch count all match the expected signals; FAIL if any one of them differs, including a second `run_extended` event or a missing snapshot.

---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Extend the completed deep-research run in the fixture spec folder by three iterations with --extend=3 --convergence-mode=off, so the converged run cannot re-stop at once, then confirm the original session identifier, the single run_extended event and the synthesis snapshot.`

### Commands

1. `rg -n -- '--extend' .skilled/commands/deep/research.md .skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-research.yaml`
2. `rg -n 'run_extended' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/lib`
3. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run append-mode-event-legacy-seam.vitest workflow-restart-lifecycle.vitest`
4. `/deep:research "<fixture topic>" :auto --spec-folder=<completed-fixture-spec-folder> --extend=3 --convergence-mode=off`
5. `rg -c '"event":"extended"' <completed-fixture-spec-folder>/research/deep-research-state.jsonl` (the `run_extended` stem lives in the ledger, and the state log carries its projected `extended` row)
6. `jq '{maxIterations, sessionId: .lineage.sessionId, generation: .lineage.generation, lineageMode: .lineage.lineageMode, continuedFromRun: .lineage.continuedFromRun}' <completed-fixture-spec-folder>/research/deep-research-config.json` (research keeps the lineage fields under `.lineage`)
7. `find <completed-fixture-spec-folder> -name 'synthesis-v*-r*.md'`

### Expected

Command 1 prints the `--extend` binding and the extension step. Command 2 prints the `run_extended` stem and its payload type. Command 3 exits 0 with no failed test. After command 4 finishes, command 5 prints `1`, command 6 shows the original `sessionId`, generation 1, `maxIterations` at the prior count plus 3 and `lineageMode` `completed-continue`, and command 7 lists one snapshot file. The run adds exactly three iteration files.

### Evidence

Capture the command 1 and 2 matches, the command 3 summary line, the command 5 count, the command 6 JSON and the command 7 listing, together with the iteration file count before and after the run.

### Pass / Fail

- **Pass**: One `run_extended` event, the original `sessionId` and generation, `maxIterations` at the prior count plus 3, a `completed-continue` lineage, one synthesis snapshot, no `resumed` row, and exactly three new iterations.
- **Fail**: Any second `run_extended` event, a changed `sessionId` or generation, a different iteration cap, a missing snapshot, an overwritten snapshot, or a dispatch count other than three.

### Failure Triage

Check the state log first, then the config. A missing event with a correct config means the gateway append was skipped, so read the extension step in `deep-research.yaml` before the classifier override. A correct event with a wrong dispatch count points at the classifier, which should land on resume.

### Optional Supplemental Checks

- Refusal: run the same command with `--extend=0` and confirm it stops with a named usage error before any state write. The flag accepts positive integers only.
- Refusal: run it against a run that is still in progress and confirm it refuses because the run is not complete.

version: "1.2.3.0"

# system-deep-loop: Manual Testing Playbook

<!-- MANUAL_PLAYBOOK_RESULT_PERSISTENCE_CONTRACT -->
> **Result persistence**: a scenario run is complete only after its `PASS`, `FAIL`, or `SKIP`
> outcome and reason are persisted into
> `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/benchmark/reports/<dated-run-label>/`.

> **EXECUTION POLICY**: Every scenario MUST be executed against the live `system-deep-loop` skill and its real `mode-registry.json` source of truth. No mocks, no stubs, and no invented routing behavior. Acceptable verdicts are PASS, FAIL, or SKIP with a documented sandbox blocker.

This document combines the manual-validation contract for the `system-deep-loop` parent-skill hub into a single operator reference. The root playbook acts as the directory, review protocol, and orchestration guide. Per-feature files provide the deeper execution contract for each scenario, including the user request, expected mode, command, agent, backend, artifact root, registry evidence, and binary validation criteria.

---

This playbook package adopts the typed per-scenario split-document pattern. The root document acts as the directory, review surface, and orchestration guide; each per-scenario file in the category folders carries typed routing gold in its YAML frontmatter plus the full execution contract in its body.

Canonical package artifacts:
- `manual-testing-playbook.md`
- `mode-routing/`
- `advisor-integration/`
- `runtime-and-backend/`
- `state-and-convergence-discipline/`

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This playbook validates the `system-deep-loop` parent-skill hub through the operator-scenario set enumerated in Section 6 (Category Index) and Section 8 (Scenario Directory), which are the source of truth for the current scenario and category totals. Each scenario keeps its stable `{PREFIX}-NNN` ID in a dedicated per-scenario file whose YAML frontmatter carries the typed routing gold and whose body holds the full execution contract. A separate `compiled-routing/` directory carries one routing-gold bundle (`DL-CR-001`) outside this operator-scenario set; it is exempt from this playbook's contract and scored elsewhere.

Coverage note: the playbook covers the hub's registry-driven routing at version `2.0.0.0`. It exercises:
- Dominant-intent routing for the lexical modes: `research` and `review`.
- Explicit mode-hint override routing, such as `research: <request>`.
- Advisor integration: the single advisor identity `system-deep-loop`, lexical scoring, and no false fire on ordinary code-edit prompts.
- The three-tier discriminator: `workflowMode`, `runtimeLoopType`, and `backendKind`.
- State and convergence discipline: externalized state, artifact-root writes, convergence stop behavior, and hub logic boundaries.

### Realistic Test Model

1. A realistic user request is given to an orchestrator that has the `system-deep-loop` skill registered.
2. The orchestrator consults the skill advisor, command router, or explicit Skill invocation and decides whether to invoke the hub, a `/deep:*` command surface, another skill, or no deep-loop workflow.
3. The operator captures: which skill won the advisor vote, which `workflowMode` was selected, which command and agent surface would be used, which backend was resolved, which artifact root was named, and what the AI's response actually was.
4. The scenario passes only when the routing matches `mode-registry.json`, the source evidence is quoted or cited, and the user-visible outcome is sound for a real operator.

### What Each Feature File Should Explain

- The realistic user request that should trigger the behavior.
- The expected mode, command, agent, backend, and artifact root.
- Why that route is expected, quoting the matching `aliases`, `advisorRouting`, `command`, `agent`, `runtimeLoopType`, `backendKind`, or `artifactRoot` fields from `mode-registry.json`.
- The expected packet and whether it owns mode behavior or the hub only routes to it.
- The pass/fail criteria with binary grading.
- Failure triage steps.

---

## 2. GLOBAL PRECONDITIONS

1. Working directory is the repository root.
2. The hub skill is present at `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/`.
3. `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/SKILL.md` states that `mode-registry.json` is the single source of truth and that the hub holds no per-mode convergence, state, or synthesis logic.
4. `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/mode-registry.json` contains exactly 2 active modes in its `modes` array.
5. The skill advisor at `.skilled/skills/system-skill-advisor/runtime/scripts/skill_advisor.py` is callable when advisor scenarios are executed.
6. The orchestrator runtime can invoke `Skill(system-deep-loop)` and can run `/deep:*` command prompts, or the operator can capture equivalent dry-run routing transcripts.
7. Operator evidence is written under `/tmp/dlw-<SCENARIO-ID>/` and never into project source paths unless a scenario explicitly states the mode's real artifact-root contract.
8. Multi-scenario waves cap at 5 parallel advisor probes. State-discipline checks that may create artifacts run serially.

---

## 3. GLOBAL EVIDENCE REQUIREMENTS

- The exact user prompt that was tested.
- The skill advisor output or command invocation transcript.
- The selected `workflowMode`.
- The registry fields used to justify the route.
- The resolved command, agent, packet, backend, and artifact root.
- The AI's user-visible response.

# Autonomous Deep Research Loop

Note: `Task` is allowed for the command executor that manages the loop. The `@deep-research` agent itself is LEAF-only and does not dispatch sub-agents.

Iterative research protocol with fresh context per iteration, externalized state, and convergence detection for deep technical investigation.

Runtime path resolution: OpenCode runtime uses `.opencode/agents/*.md`, a link to the `.skilled/agents/*.md` source; Claude runtime uses `.claude/agents/*.md`. The other agent trees are listed in `.skilled/bin/agent-mirror-crosswalk.md`.

Operator contract precedence for this skill surface (highest first): command entrypoint syntax in `.skilled/commands/deep/research.md`; convergence math in `references/convergence/convergence.md` and the deep-research YAML workflow; runtime agent inventories from the checked-in runtime directories above.

## 1. WHEN TO USE

### Activation Triggers

Use this skill when:
- Deep investigation requiring multiple rounds of discovery
- Topic spans 3+ technical domains or sources
- Initial findings need progressive refinement
- Overnight or unattended research sessions
- Research where prior findings inform subsequent queries

Keyword triggers:

- `autoresearch`
- `deep research`
- `autonomous research`
- `research loop`
- `iterative research`
- `multi-round research`
- `deep investigation`
- `comprehensive research`

# Implementation Summary

<!-- SPECKIT_LEVEL: 3 -->
<!-- HVR_REFERENCE: .skilled/skills/sk-doc/sk-create-with-human-voice/references/hvr-rules.md -->

| **Level** | 3 |
<!-- /ANCHOR:metadata -->

---

<!-- ANCHOR:what-built -->
## What Was Built

`--depth-mode=expansive` lets a research run grow its own questions. It seeds depth-1 nodes, admits leaf proposals under six fixed checks, steers to the shallowest open node, refuses a soft stop while nodes are open and closes every node cleanly. A run without the flag, or with `--depth-mode=default`, renders the same registry, dashboard, focus, prompt pack, lineage prompt and stop config as before.

### Opt-in expansive depth mode for deep-research

`frontier-admit.cjs` is the only writer of `research/frontier-nodes.jsonl` and the frontier inbox rows. The reducer projects the store as `registry.frontier`, resolves a question when its node closes and applies the 50/35/15 depth budget. The stop decision vetoes a soft stop with `frontierOpen` after the iteration floor. Fan-out deals the seeded nodes across lineages and the merge checks the partition.

| `.codex/agents/deep-research.toml` | `frontier_proposal` leaf-contract row |
| `.dsh/agents/deep-research.patch.yml` | `frontier_proposal` leaf-contract row |
| `.pi/agents/deep-research.md` | `frontier_proposal` leaf-contract row |

<!-- ANCHOR:what-built -->
## What Was Built
<!-- FILL: Open with the user-visible change in two sentences, then list each changed file. GOOD: `--keep 3` leaves three archives in `logs/archive`. A plain run leaves all ten. WEAK: Implemented the feature. -->
With `--depth-mode=expansive`, a review run seeds depth-1 sub-target nodes from its target, keeps their status in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, works the active node's next uncovered dimension each iteration and refuses a stop candidate while any node is open. Without the flag, a review writes what it wrote before, except for one expansive-only paragraph in CLI lineage prompts, which is waived pending operator ratification. A golden test pins seven default-path artifacts to bytes captured before the build.

T002, 008's support decision: "Support custom dimensions in `buildDimensionCoverage` (ADR-001, OD-001) | Operator choice: every configured dimension is tracked and can be covered", so the per-node dimension universe is the configured `reviewDimensions` list.

### Opt-in expansive depth mode for deep-review

The admission helper gained a review target mode (no inbox, review config caps, scope inside a file-list target, seed prune reasons, block by node id). The review reducer projects `registry.frontier` with per-node dimension coverage, the depth budget, the active node and its next dimension; open nodes are `searchDebt`, terminal nodes go to `candidateCoverage` per ADR-001. The workflow seeds, activates, admits, steers and closes stuck nodes; the stop decision vetoes review stop candidates while nodes are open; the review pivot seeds from unanswered nodes and refuses an open frontier; fan-out partitions depth-1 nodes and merges lineage frontiers behind a partition guard.

**Shared backend contracts (consolidation promotions).** Beyond the numbered entries above, the backend hosts a small set of generic plumbing the consumer modes import rather than duplicate: `lib/deep-loop/runtime-capabilities.cjs` (parameterized capability resolver, with byte-compatible per-skill shims), `lib/deep-loop/artifact-root.cjs` (canonical seam re-exporting `resolveArtifactRoot` from `system-spec-kit/shared/review-research-paths.cjs`), `lib/deep-loop/lifecycle-taxonomy.cjs` (terminal lifecycle enum: seven `stopReason` plus four `sessionOutcome` values) and `scripts/loop-lock.cjs` (CLI adapter over `loop-lock.ts`). These contracts register no MCP tools and carry no public workflow routing; resource-map emission stays in the workflow shared-synthesis layer, not in this runtime.

=== release notes deep loop section ===
## Deep Loop

Deep loops run research and review passes on their own until their evidence says stop. Their verdicts and stop calls now come from scripts instead of judgment, so a rerun on the same state always agrees. The AI Council also moves out into an agent of its own.

#### The AI Council Is Its Own Agent <a id="c-the-ai-council-is-its-own-agent"></a>

> [!WARNING]
> `/deep:ai-council` is gone. Run `/speckit:ai-council` instead.

- It binds the spec folder and dispatches `@ai-council` once.
- The agent runs three rounds by default.
- It writes only under the packet's `ai-council/` folder.

#### A Review Fails While a Required Check Fails <a id="c-a-review-fails-while-a-required-check-fails"></a>

- A review returns FAIL when a P0 is open or a required check fails.
- The required checks are evidence, scope and coverage.
- Scripts compute the checks and the verdict.
- Coverage reads every shape a review records.
- The report carries the counts the verdict used.
- Open search debt makes a review CONDITIONAL.

#### Loops Stop the Same Way Every Time <a id="c-loops-stop-the-same-way-every-time"></a>

- `convergence.cjs --stop-decision` makes the stop call as one `stop_decision` object.
- The review vote follows your `convergenceThreshold`.
- A resume rebuilds its counts from the state log.
- Three failed passes in a row end the loop with reason `error`.
- `--restart` and `--dry-run` work on research and review.
- `maxDurationMinutes` ends a run on time.
- A review fan-out takes `--review-target`.
- Each deep command runs from one workflow file.

<details>
<summary><b>Smaller fixes</b></summary>

- Vibe runs bill your Vibe plan through your `vibe --setup` sign-in.
- A quota refusal counts as `quota`, not a crash.
- `fanout-run.cjs` refuses a one-lineage run.

[0m$ [0mcd /Users/michelkerkmeester/worktrees/public/455-expansive-depth-integration && sed -n '230,245p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/SKILL.md; echo "=== convergence ref cap ==="; grep -n "maxChildrenPerNode" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/convergence/convergence.md .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md; echo "=== code root caps ==="; sed -n '52,64p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs
The stuck count and the consecutive failure count are rebuilt from the state log on every pass, so a resumed run counts the same as one that never paused. Failed iterations stay out of the ratio signals. Three trailing failed iterations stop the loop with stop reason `error`. In confirm mode only, the workflow first asks the operator Retry, Skip or Cancel. Auto mode has no such question.

Each pass also compares `maxDurationMinutes` with the config `createdAt`. When the limit is reached the loop skips the dispatch and goes to synthesis with stop reason `maxDurationReached`.

### Expansive Depth Mode

- `--depth-mode=expansive` keeps a depth-capped frontier of sub-target nodes beside the review target. An absent flag means `default`, which runs exactly as before.
- The flag writes `antiConvergence.depthMode` and the caps in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split across the depth bands, and `maxFrontierNodes`, set to `maxIterations` at config creation. `antiConvergence.depthMode` is locked against the optimizer; the `expansiveDepth` caps are in neither its tunable nor its locked list.
- `step_frontier_seed` writes the depth-1 nodes before iteration 1 through `runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs seed --target review`, after `step_create_config` or before `step_fanout_spawn` on a fan-out run. A merge with no diff of its own is pruned `merge-no-own-diff` and an index rebuild `generated-artifact`.
- `step_frontier_admit` runs before the reducer and prunes a `frontier_proposal` on the first failed check among relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle. Node status lives in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, written only by `frontier-admit.cjs`; review has no inbox.
- `step_read_state` reads the active node and sets `next_dimension` to its next uncovered dimension, `step_frontier_activate` marks it active before dispatch, and the prompt pack's `{frontier_block}` names the node. Next Focus takes the blocked stop, then the pivot, then the node, then the leaf focus, then the first uncovered dimension.
- While a node stays open the frontier veto turns a stop candidate into `CONTINUE` with reasonDetail `frontierOpen`. It never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the review floor, and stuck recovery closes the active node `exhausted`.
- A fan-out run deals the depth-1 nodes round-robin in node-id order, binds `frontier_scope:` beside `review_focus:`, records the partition as `frontier_partition` in `orchestration-summary.json` and returns a partition guard for any node no lineage reports. Size each lineage's iteration cap (the `--iters=N` value on its executor entry, which `fanout-run.cjs` passes as that lineage's `--max-iterations`, 12 when unset) so half of it covers `ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions`.

---

# Quality guards

## 1. OVERVIEW

Blocks premature stops until source diversity, focus alignment, and weak-source checks all pass.

Quality guards sit after the convergence vote and before synthesis. They make sure a statistically quiet loop still has enough evidence quality and enough question coverage to stop safely.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

The legal-stop bundle is explicit. convergence.cjs checks that each key question has evidence-backed coverage, that research stayed aligned with the original question set, and that the answered set is not leaning on a single tentative source. A stop is legal only when both the convergence gate and these guard checks pass together.

Guard failures are visible packet events, not quiet side effects. convergence.cjs reports the failed guard as `qualityGuardFailed` and names it in `stop_decision.blockedBy`, and the workflow logs the failed guard names when it continues. The `blocked_stop` event carries gate results, blockers, recovery guidance, session identifier, and generation so the reducer and dashboard can show why the loop had to continue.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/references/convergence/convergence.md` | Reference | Defines the legal-stop gate bundle and the source-diversity, focus-alignment, and weak-source rules. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md` | Reference | Defines when the guards run and how blocked-stop events are persisted. |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-research.yaml` | Workflow | Persists `blocked_stop` events and logs failed guard names. Confirm mode adds approval gates. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/scripts/reduce-state.cjs` | Reducer | Surfaces blocked-stop history and recovery hints into strategy and dashboard outputs. |

title: "Completed-run extension (--extend)"
description: "A completed deep-research run extends in place by N iterations under --extend=N, keeps its session identifier and generation, and records one run_extended event."
trigger_phrases:
  - "--extend"
  - "--extend=N"
  - "extend completed research run"
  - "run_extended"
  - "--extend-minutes"
version: 1.0.1.0
---

# Completed-run extension (--extend)

## 1. OVERVIEW

`--extend=N` reopens a completed research run in the same session and generation and adds `N` iterations. The run keeps its `sessionId` and generation, records one `run_extended` ledger event, and keeps the prior synthesis as `research/synthesis-v{generation}-r{continuedFromRun}.md`. `--extend-minutes=M` adds minutes to the duration budget of the same extension.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Lineage And Preconditions

The extension uses lineage mode `completed-continue`. The run must already be complete. An unfinished run continues with `--lineage-mode=resume` instead. The extension is refused with fan-out.

### Iteration And Duration Budget

The iteration cap rises by `N` over the prior count. `--extend-minutes=M` is valid only with `--extend`. The new duration budget is `max(prior, elapsed) + M`, because the clock counts time since `createdAt`, so an extension started after the old budget still runs.

### Stop Interaction

Under `stopPolicy: convergence`, an extension with nothing new can stop again at once on `all_questions_answered`. Pass `--convergence-mode=off`, use `stopPolicy: max-iterations`, or add questions to `research/inbox.jsonl`.

### Ledger And Reducer Routing

The `run_extended` stem is part of the deep-research ledger schema, and the deep-research reducer routes it. The lifecycle event filters in `deep-research/scripts/reduce-state.cjs` read the same event.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/commands/deep/research.md` | Command doc | Flag rows for `--extend` and `--extend-minutes`. |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-research.yaml` | Command workflow | Lineage handling and the snapshot of the prior synthesis. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/lib/deep-research-ledger-schema/deep-research-ledger-schema.ts` | Shared | Defines the `run_extended` stem and its payload. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/lib/deep-research-reducers/deep-research-reducer.ts` | Shared | Routes the `run_extended` stem. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/scripts/reduce-state.cjs` | Script | Lifecycle event filters that read the extension event. |

### Validation And Tests

| File | Type | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/unit/deep-research-reducers.vitest.ts` | Vitest | Reducer routing for the run lifecycle event stems. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/manual-testing-playbook/entry-points-and-modes/extend-completed-research-run.md` | Manual playbook | DR-065: same-session continuation, the `run_extended` event and the synthesis snapshot. |

| `--lineage-mode` | `auto` \| `resume` \| `restart` | Session lifecycle intent, default `auto`. `resume` keeps the same `sessionId` and archives nothing. `restart` opens a new `sessionId` at generation+1 and archives the prior `research/`. `fork` is not accepted. |
| `--extend` | positive integer, as `--extend=N` | Extends a completed run in place by `N` iterations, lineage mode `completed-continue`. The new budget is `max(prior, elapsed) + M`, because the clock counts idle time since `createdAt`, so an extension started after the old budget still runs. Valid only with `--extend`. |
| `--executor` | `TYPE` | Selects the executor kind, and is repeatable. Which of those a given kind supports, and the fail-fast on an unsupported pairing, are in the presentation asset. |

| `--concurrency` | `N` | Caps concurrent lineages in the fan-out pool. Default `2`. |

// The frontier focus names the node and never repeats its question text: a
// question resolves when an iteration's focus equals its text, so echoing the
// text would close a node just by visiting it.
// The leaf writes its own focus line and may reword it, so the node marker counts
// wherever it appears; the id must end there, so F-1-001 never matches F-1-0010.
function frontierNodeVisits(nodeId, iterationRecords) {
  const escaped = String(nodeId).replace(/[.*+?^${}()|[\]\\]/g, '\\$&');
  const marker = new RegExp(`(?:^|[^\\w-])Frontier node ${escaped}(?![\\w-])`);
  return (Array.isArray(iterationRecords) ? iterationRecords : [])
    .filter((record) => typeof record?.focus === 'string' && marker.test(record.focus))
    .length;
}

function pickFrontierFocus(frontier, iterationRecords) {
  const open = (Array.isArray(frontier?.nodes) ? frontier.nodes : [])
    .filter((node) => node.status === 'open' || node.status === 'active');
  if (open.length === 0) return '';
  // Breadth first: the shallowest node, then one not yet visited, then the oldest.
  const ranked = open
    .map((node) => ({ node, visits: frontierNodeVisits(node.nodeId, iterationRecords) }))
    .sort((left, right) => (left.node.depth - right.node.depth)
      || (left.visits - right.visits)
      || ((left.node.iteration ?? 0) - (right.node.iteration ?? 0))
      || left.node.nodeId.localeCompare(right.node.nodeId));
  const chosen = ranked[0].node;
  const scope = Array.isArray(chosen.scope) ? chosen.scope.join(', ') : (chosen.scope || 'unscoped');
  return `Frontier node ${chosen.nodeId} (depth ${chosen.depth}, scope ${scope}): ${chosen.question}`;
}

// Depth bands share maxIterations 50/35/15 by default (depths 0-1, 2, 3). A band's
// unspent share rolls down to the next band only once the band has no open node,
// and never up. When a band's spend reaches its share, its open nodes close as
// exhausted with reason budget, so the run stops waiting on work it cannot fund.
// An open node whose question an iteration already answered is marked answered
// first: the budget closes only work still outstanding, and an answered node
// never holds its band open against the roll-down.

=== HEAD v2.1.0.0 lines 20-30 ===
    20	
    21	- **Completed runs take `--extend=N`.** `/deep:research --extend=N` and `/deep:review --extend=N` add N iterations to a completed run in the same session and generation.
    22	- **`--extend-minutes=M` adds time.** The flag raises the run's time budget by M minutes. Both flags accept positive integers only.
    23	- **Each extension is one ledger event.** The `run_extended` event records the extension, and the run re-synthesizes after the new iterations.
    24	- **The prior synthesis is kept.** The synthesis or review report is copied to a versioned snapshot before the first new iteration. An existing snapshot is never overwritten.
    25	- **Unsafe extensions are refused.** A run that is not complete, a fan-out config, or a value that is not a positive integer stops with a named error.

=== 010-research-frontier-mode spec status ===
23:Deep-research treats a run that answers its 3-7 init questions narrowly as converged, because every stop signal and focus source is anchored to the question set written at init and nothing grows that set mid-run. This phase builds Slice J of the parent research: an opt-in `--depth-mode=expansive` that seeds depth-1 frontier nodes before the first iteration, admits typed `frontier_proposal` rows through a workflow-owned admission step into `research/inbox.jsonl`, keeps node status in a durable append-only store, projects a `frontier` field into the findings registry, picks Next Focus by lowest open depth, adds a `frontierOpen` stop veto and closes terminal nodes so the run can still stop cleanly. Fan-out runs get the seed before any lineage spawns, a `--depth-mode` passthrough, a persisted partition and a merged `frontier`. The proof is a golden test that keeps default runs byte-identical, an admission table test, a stop-decision table test, a close test and a pilot run on the skill-advisor chat auto-utilization target rooted at the prompt-policy gate.
25:**Key Decisions**: a separate `--depth-mode` flag rather than a fifth `--convergence-mode` value; frontier growth travels through delta and inbox rows, never the research state-log upcast; node status lives in its own append-only store because the inbox keeps only six fields and the registry is rebuilt on every reduce.
