    48	<!-- FILL: Open with the user-visible change in two sentences, then list each changed file. GOOD: `--keep 3` leaves three archives in `logs/archive`. A plain run leaves all ten. WEAK: Implemented the feature. -->
    49	With `--depth-mode=expansive`, a review run seeds depth-1 sub-target nodes from its target, keeps their status in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, works the active node's next uncovered dimension each iteration and refuses a stop candidate while any node is open. Without the flag, a review writes what it wrote before, except for one expansive-only paragraph in CLI lineage prompts, which is waived pending operator ratification. A golden test pins seven default-path artifacts to bytes captured before the build.
    50	
    51	T002, 008's support decision: "Support custom dimensions in `buildDimensionCoverage` (ADR-001, OD-001) | Operator choice: every configured dimension is tracked and can be covered", so the per-node dimension universe is the configured `reviewDimensions` list.
    52	
    53	### Opt-in expansive depth mode for deep-review
    54	
    55	The admission helper gained a review target mode (no inbox, review config caps, scope inside a file-list target, seed prune reasons, block by node id). The review reducer projects `registry.frontier` with per-node dimension coverage, the depth budget, the active node and its next dimension; open nodes are `searchDebt`, terminal nodes go to `candidateCoverage` per ADR-001. The workflow seeds, activates, admits, steers and closes stuck nodes; the stop decision vetoes review stop candidates while nodes are open; the review pivot seeds from unanswered nodes and refuses an open frontier; fan-out partitions depth-1 nodes and merges lineage frontiers behind a partition guard.

    88	## Key Decisions
    89	<!-- FILL: Write one decision per row. Give the reason, not the label. GOOD: Option over environment variable: one run can pick a different count without editing config. WEAK: Used the best approach. -->
    90	| Decision | Why |
    91	|----------|-----|
    92	| Admit into `searchDebt` and `candidateCoverage`, not an inbox | The review reducer has no question surface, so an inbox row would be read by nothing |
    93	| Reuse the phase-010 admission order, budget and veto | One frontier design for both loops keeps the bounds and stop semantics identical |
    94	| Attribute iterations by record field, bounded marker or store activation row (D-011-01) | A leaf that rewords its focus still spends the node's visit, budget and dimension |
    95	| Veto review hard stop candidates (D-011-02) | `all_dimensions_clean` is a hard stop, so the research guard would never veto it |
    96	| Strip the expansive-only persona paragraph from the golden lineage prompt (ADR-005) | The leaf contract must change the persona the lineage prompt embeds; waived pending operator ratification, as 010's ADR-006 |
    97	| Pilot keeps all four dimensions and raises `maxIterations` (T000) | Narrowing dimensions would weaken the coverage the review gate checks |

This was the one planned rerun; no further pilot runs tonight.

- AC-014: FAIL. Stats `{"total":16,"open":0,"active":0,"answered":16,...}`, stop `maxIterationsReached` at check 17 (`blockedBy:["sourceDiversity"]`, graph `STOP_BLOCKED`).
- Proposed amended criterion (a stop candidate forms before the cap): FAIL. All questions answered with an empty frontier first happened at check 17, the cap check. Checks 15 and 16 had an empty frontier but 16/19 and 17/19 answered.
- Structural cause: `deep-research.yaml` sets `maxFrontierNodes = max_iterations`. Seed 4 + 12 admissions in iterations 1-3 filled all 16 slots, each iteration answered one node, and the three init questions waited until iterations 15-16 because a frontier node outranks strategy focus. A non-cap stop is unreachable while the node cap equals the iteration cap.
- Fixes confirmed live: iteration 1 got a frontier block (fix c); `step_frontier_graph_upsert` exited 0 on all 16 iterations (fix a); all 16 nodes ended answered (fix b).

function applyFrontierBudget(nodes, iterationRecords, maxIterations, budgetSplit, answeredNodeIds = new Set()) {
  const split = budgetSplit && typeof budgetSplit === 'object' ? budgetSplit : {};
  const shares = [
    Number.isFinite(split.depth1) ? split.depth1 : 0.5,
    Number.isFinite(split.depth2) ? split.depth2 : 0.35,
    Number.isFinite(split.depth3) ? split.depth3 : 0.15,
  ];
  const cap = Number.isFinite(maxIterations) && maxIterations > 0 ? maxIterations : 10;
  const bandOf = (depth) => (depth <= 1 ? 0 : depth === 2 ? 1 : 2);
  const spent = [0, 0, 0];
  for (const node of nodes) {
    const visits = frontierNodeVisits(node.nodeId, (Array.isArray(iterationRecords) ? iterationRecords : [])
      .filter((record) => !['error', 'failed', 'failure', 'timeout'].includes(String(record?.status || '').toLowerCase())));
    spent[bandOf(node.depth)] += visits;
  }
  let result = nodes.map((node) => ((node.status === 'open' || node.status === 'active') && answeredNodeIds.has(node.nodeId)
    ? { ...node, status: 'answered', reason: null }
    : { ...node }));
  const budget = {};
  let carry = 0;
  for (let band = 0; band < 3; band += 1) {
    const allotted = cap * shares[band] + carry;
    if (spent[band] >= allotted) {
      result = result.map((node) => (bandOf(node.depth) === band && (node.status === 'open' || node.status === 'active')
        ? { ...node, status: 'exhausted', reason: 'budget' }
        : node));
    }
    const bandOpen = result.some((node) => bandOf(node.depth) === band && (node.status === 'open' || node.status === 'active'));
    carry = bandOpen ? 0 : Math.max(0, allotted - spent[band]);
    budget[`depth${band + 1}`] = { allotted: Math.round(allotted * 100) / 100, spent: spent[band] };
=== review twin 1945-1960 ===
      .filter((iteration) => iteration !== null),
  );
  return (Array.isArray(iterationRecords) ? iterationRecords : []).filter((record) => {
    if (!record || record.type !== 'iteration') return false;
    if (record.frontierNodeId === nodeId) return true;
    if (typeof record.focus === 'string' && marker.test(record.focus)) return true;
    const iteration = getIterationRun(record);
    return iteration !== null && activeIterations.has(iteration);
  });
}

// Only an attempt that produced evidence counts as a visit: an errored or timed
// out record is retried work, not depth the node has already spent.
function reviewFrontierVisits(nodeId, history, iterationRecords) {
  return reviewRecordsForNode(nodeId, history, iterationRecords)
    .filter((record) => !FRONTIER_FAILED_STATUSES.has(String(record.status || '').toLowerCase()))
    .length;
}

// unspent share rolls down to the next band only once the band has no open node,

  const shouldUseIdeaFocus = !normalizedFocus
    || normalizedFocus === '[none yet]'
    || normalizedFocus === '[all tracked questions are resolved]';

    21	- **Completed runs take `--extend=N`.** `/deep:research --extend=N` and `/deep:review --extend=N` add N iterations to a completed run in the same session and generation.
    22	- **`--extend-minutes=M` adds time.** The flag raises the run's time budget by M minutes. Both flags accept positive integers only.
    23	- **Each extension is one ledger event.** The `run_extended` event records the extension, and the run re-synthesizes after the new iterations.
    24	- **The prior synthesis is kept.** The synthesis or review report is copied to a versioned snapshot before the first new iteration. An existing snapshot is never overwritten.
    25	- **Unsafe extensions are refused.** A run that is not complete, a fan-out config, or a value that is not a positive integer stops with a named error.

#### Description
verify that a running deep review iteration writes a `graphEvents` array that includes review graph nodes such as `dimension_node`, `file_node`, and `finding_node`.

#### Scenario Contract
Prompt: `Validate deep-review graphEvents records for dimension, file, and finding nodes in graph-aware review convergence.`

Expected signals: `graphEvents` referenced as iteration-record input for graph-aware review convergence. Replay tests include review node-type coverage for `dimension_node`, `file_node`, and `finding_node`.

#### Test Execution
> **Feature File:** [DRV-015](../manual-testing-playbook/iteration-execution-and-state-discipline/graph-events-review.md)
--- scenario ---
- Title: Review iterations emit structured graphEvents.
- Given: A running deep review iteration.
- When: The iteration completes with P0/P1/P2 findings.
- Then: The JSONL record contains a `graphEvents` array. The coverage-graph reducer ingests those events as `loop_type='review'` nodes/edges scoped to the session and iteration.
- Real user request: When a review iteration finishes, what graph data is written so coverage and convergence can replay the review state?
- Prompt: `Validate deep-review graphEvents records and confirm the coverage-graph reducer ingests them under loop_type=review.`
- Expected execution process: Inspect the deep-review convergence reference for the graph-aware iteration-record contract first, then the coverage-graph database source for the active `loop_type` schema, then the live coverage-graph tests for ingestion coverage.
- Desired user-facing outcome: The user understands that completed review iterations emit replayable graph events and that the coverage-graph reducer keys them by `loop_type='review'`, session, and iteration.
- Expected signals: `graphEvents` referenced as iteration-record input for graph-aware review convergence. The active `coverage-graph-db.ts` source carries `LoopType = 'research' | 'review'` and persists nodes/edges scoped to `(spec_folder, loop_type, session_id, iteration)`. Live reducer tests exercise the review path.
- Pass/fail posture: PASS if the convergence reference, the active coverage-graph source, and the live reducer tests agree that review iterations carry `graphEvents` and the reducer keys ingestion by `loop_type='review'`. FAIL if the record contract is absent or the active source no longer supports the review loop type.

## What's New at a Glance

- Research accepts `--claude-config-dir` on `cli-claude-code` runs.
- The dir is checked before the prompt is read. A dir outside the allowed roots is refused.
- Claude runs under the configured dir. It overrides an inherited `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`.
- The audit record names the dir the run used.
- A run that names no dir behaves as before.

&nbsp;

## How It Was Checked

New cases cover the flag, its validation and the audit record. All ten dispatch cases pass in a detached run. Inside a Claude session, two skip under the Claude ancestry guard.

&nbsp;

## Upgrade

No migration required. Set `configDir` in the run's executor config to pin the account. An inherited `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` still reaches a research run that names no dir. Stripping it would change every default run, so it stays as it is.

&nbsp;
